## 119. Abby round 3, seed 41 - the judges' sc12 Picture 1, tested and rejected on lettering

![Abby round 3, seed 41 - the judges' sc12 Picture 1, tested and rejected on lettering](images/119-abby-round-3-seed-41-the-judges-sc12-picture-1-tested-and-rejected-on-lettering.png)

**The ask.** Round 3 of perfecting Abby. The judges called the root cause: rounds 1 and 2 sent a BUST as Picture 1, and qwen-image-edit copies Picture 1's staging, so 6 of 6 came back as portraits on bare paper while the prompt asked for the counter, both hands and the towel. Picture 1 must be a FULL PLATE. Build the eye tile by hand from look-card section 2 and send it as Picture 3 (Step 5, skipped until now); drop canon/vision/studies/abby.png, the source of the level lip line and the animal eye. Fix the jawline, the mouth, the eye, the nose, the ears, the hands and towel, the occlusion and the gaze as EDIT lines. Hold the recipe.

**The thinking.** The judges named canon/showcase-retired/sc12-permanent-receipt.png as Picture 1: the whole art field, caption band cut, resized to the house 4:5. I built exactly that (caption band measured, not guessed - every row from y=1500 down is a flat 245, so the cut is y=1498) and ran it before anything else, because the whole round rests on it. It half-works and half-fails, and the half that fails is fatal, so it is filed as evidence rather than binned.

**Settings.** local/qwen-image-edit-2511, 4:5 1344x1680, fast Lightning 8 steps, cfg 1, euler/simple, shift 3.0, 3 references (Picture 1 the approved blank plate canon/plates/trio.png cut x60..1140 y20..1370 to a true 4:5; Picture 2 canon/characters/abby/kit/bust.png for the collar hardware and the blouse only; Picture 3 the hand-drawn eye chart abby-eye-tile.png), scripts/cast-study.py --cast-count 3 --keep-duo (Picture 1 = the sc12 art field, 1344x1680)

**Prompt.** [prompts/119-abby-round-3-seed-41-the-judges-sc12-picture-1-tested-and-rejected-on-lettering.prompt.txt](prompts/119-abby-round-3-seed-41-the-judges-sc12-picture-1-tested-and-rejected-on-lettering.prompt.txt)

**Verdict.** THE DIAGNOSIS IS RIGHT AND THE PLATE IS WRONG. Right: a full-plate Picture 1 brings the whole room back at once - the counter, the back bar, all three characters, both of Abby's hands, the towel, the occlusion at her waist. That is the round-2 failure cured in one move. Wrong: sc12 is a PUBLISHED strip and it is covered in lettering - a live television chyron, a lettered chalkboard and a lettered receipt in Barclay's hands. At cfg 1 the model copied all three and then invented more of it: the chyron came back as 'PPICE GAUES CONES IN AS EXPECTEO', the bottle labels grew scribble, and the receipt kept its columns. Pseudo-text is what INSPECTION.md check 17 calls fatal. Two further drifts: the camera pulled back off the marble into stools and floor, and the left wall grew invented posters. The fix is not to fight the plate with words - it is to send a plate that has nothing to copy. canon/plates/trio.png is the APPROVED BLANK PLATE and carries the identical construction with the screen already off, the slate already wiped, every label already blank and no receipt. Picture 1 changed to trio.png for the rest of the round.

*Logged 13:37.*

---

## 120. Abby round 3 - her eye, drawn by hand (look-card Step 5, skipped until now)

![Abby round 3 - her eye, drawn by hand (look-card Step 5, skipped until now)](images/120-abby-round-3-her-eye-drawn-by-hand-look-card-step-5-skipped-until-now.png)

**The ask.** Round 3 of perfecting Abby. The judges called the root cause: rounds 1 and 2 sent a BUST as Picture 1, and qwen-image-edit copies Picture 1's staging, so 6 of 6 came back as portraits on bare paper while the prompt asked for the counter, both hands and the towel. Picture 1 must be a FULL PLATE. Build the eye tile by hand from look-card section 2 and send it as Picture 3 (Step 5, skipped until now); drop canon/vision/studies/abby.png, the source of the level lip line and the animal eye. Fix the jawline, the mouth, the eye, the nose, the ears, the hands and towel, the occlusion and the gaze as EDIT lines. Hold the recipe.

**The thinking.** Look-card section 1: 'Her corrected eye must be HAND-BUILT once from the section 2 table and become her permanent paste source (Step 5), not copied from any existing picture.' Every tile in the set carries the failing eye instead - the face reference measures 87.6% and 83.5% of each opening below luminance 60, the definitive study 52.3% and 39.7%, and none of them puts white at either side of the iris. A correct eye cannot arrive from a reference that does not contain one, at any seed, which is why six seeds produced the same eye six times. So it is drawn, not sourced: scripts/make-abby-eye-tile.py builds ONE eye in a 512 cell from the section 2 wording and mirrors it for the pair, so both eyes are the same size, shape and height by construction. All line work - the iris mid-tone is made of radiating strokes, not a grey wash - and no lettering anywhere on the tile, because a label would be copied into the picture as pseudo-text.

**Settings.** PIL, drawn at 3x and downsampled, 1024x470, greyscale; sent as Picture 3

**Prompt.** [prompts/120-abby-round-3-her-eye-drawn-by-hand-look-card-step-5-skipped-until-now.prompt.txt](prompts/120-abby-round-3-her-eye-drawn-by-hand-look-card-step-5-skipped-until-now.prompt.txt)

**Verdict.** It does its job and it also taught the round something. It works: the near eye across all six renders measures 23-26% below L60 against 52.3% for the best tile in the repository, with genuine paper-white on both sides of the iris. It has one cost, seen at seed 33 and faintly at 21: a diagram sent as a reference can be drawn INTO the room as a picture - seed 33 put a cartoon westie with one giant eye and a starburst inside the wiped slate. Round 4's tile needs a role line that forbids it appearing as a picture on any wall.

*Logged 13:37.*

**Settings.** local/qwen-image-edit-2511, 4:5 1344x1680, fast Lightning 8 steps, cfg 1, euler/simple, shift 3.0, 3 references (Picture 1 the approved blank plate canon/plates/trio.png cut x60..1140 y20..1370 to a true 4:5; Picture 2 canon/characters/abby/kit/bust.png for the collar hardware and the blouse only; Picture 3 the hand-drawn eye chart abby-eye-tile.png), scripts/cast-study.py --cast-count 3 --keep-duo, seed 44

**Prompt.** [prompts/126-abby-character-study-round-3-seed-44-best-of-the-round.prompt.txt](prompts/126-abby-character-study-round-3-seed-44-best-of-the-round.prompt.txt)

**Verdict.** BEST OF THE SIX, and the first Abby of the whole task that is a scene rather than a portrait. Every construction fix lands together: the room and the back bar behind her, the marble crossing her at the waist and hiding her below it, both hands working - the left closed round the bowl of the stemmed glass, the right pressing the folded towel down inside it - Drew seated frame-left and Barclay frame-right on the near side, her head high in the frame because she stands while they sit. Nothing is lettered anywhere but the mirrored window name: slate blank, every bottle label blank, no receipt, no caption, no signature. The face: the best measured eye of the round at 24.3% below L60 with white at 250 and 250 against a paper of 253 - genuine paper-white at BOTH sides of the iris, a drawn iris ring, a pupil smaller than the iris, one catchlight, lid and lash - the cleanest closed smile of the six with both corners lifted, ears matched in size and height, a flat black nose smaller than the eye opening, and the teardrop gem in its beaded bezel that the wardrobe edit asked for and earlier rounds drew as a heart. THREE FAULTS REMAIN, and they are the same three in all six, so they are language and reference faults rather than seed faults: (1) THE GAZE - she looks out at the reader, which is the founder's oldest complaint about her; the gaze edit loses to Picture 1, where she also looks out. (2) THE JAWLINE - the long show-coat still runs past the jaw down onto the throat instead of stopping dead at it. (3) THE LIP LINE opens a small dark gap right of centre. Round 4 should hold this Picture 1 and this eye tile and repair those three.

*Logged 13:37.*

## 128. Abby round 4 - the new Picture 2: the plates Rick accepted, in place of the kit tiles

![Abby round 4 - the new Picture 2: the plates Rick accepted, in place of the kit tiles](images/128-abby-round-4-the-new-picture-2-the-plates-rick-accepted-in-place-of-the-kit-tiles.png)

**The ask.** Round 4 of perfecting Abby. The judges called the references the disease, not the seeds: DROP canon/vision/studies/abby.png and canon/characters/abby/kit/bust.png outright - both draw the silky long coat, the wide lynx-tufted ears and the open-blouse cleavage that all six round-3 renders copied, and round 3 was still sending bust.png as Picture 2, which is where the Bichon came from. Replace them with crops of the plates Rick actually accepted, sc04 (470,360,820,900) and sc12 (450,350,830,930). Keep Picture 1 and the eye chart. Then fix, as EDIT lines: the eye pasted from the chart with a paper-white crescent at BOTH corners; the long fur stopping dead at the jaw; the closed-lip smile with no gap; the gaze on Drew and never on the reader; the harsh short double coat at a third the length; the broad flat-topped chrysanthemum head and blunt rectangular bearded muzzle; the small close-set erect triangular ears; the furred neck carrying the collar; the heavy black studded collar with a heart pendant; the buttoned blouse over a modest chest; grey modelling under the jaw; the back bar in wood grain with legible bottles; a clean straight edge on all four sides. And Drew, in the same plate: downy dash-strokes instead of scales, one S-curve with a single reversal, a heavy-lidded human eye. Hold the recipe - cfg 1 is correct - and re-run the same six seeds for a clean A/B.

**The thinking.** The judges were exact about the diagnosis and I agree with it: every Abby tile in the repository draws the wrong dog. bust.png and head.png both carry a long silky coat, big wide-set ears with lynx tufts and a round lapdog skull, and the model copied all of it faithfully for three rounds. The plates Rick ACCEPTED draw the right dog - sc04 and sc12 both give a broad flat-topped skull, small hard triangular ears set close on top, a short blunt rectangular muzzle with a beard fringe hanging below it, a heavy black studded collar and a buttoned blouse. So the tile is built from those two crops at the judges' own boxes. I could not send them as Picture 2 and Picture 4 as ordered, because the bridge caps the qwen family at three references and silently drops the rest off the end of the list - a fourth picture would never have reached the model. Laying both crops into one tile keeps both of them on the wire inside that cap. The four legible bottle labels inside the sc04 crop were blanked to plain paper before sending, because round 3 already paid for the lesson that lettering in a reference comes back as pseudo-text at cfg 1.

**Settings.** PIL, sc04 crop (470,360,820,900) and sc12 crop (450,350,830,930), both scaled to 760px high and laid side by side, 1027x760 greyscale; scripts/make-abby-round4-tiles.py

**Prompt.** [prompts/128-abby-round-4-the-new-picture-2-the-plates-rick-accepted-in-place-of-the-kit-tiles.prompt.txt](prompts/128-abby-round-4-the-new-picture-2-the-plates-rick-accepted-in-place-of-the-kit-tiles.prompt.txt)

**Verdict.** IT WORKED, AND IT IS THE ONE THING THAT DID. The Bichon is gone 6 of 6: every render in the round now carries the broad flat-topped skull, the small close-set triangular ears and the short blunt bearded muzzle of the accepted plates, where round 3 gave a round fluffy lapdog head with wide tufted ears in all six. The diptych did not leak - no render drew two Abbys, a gutter or a framed panel. What the tile did NOT fix, because it carries these faults itself and the EDITS could not out-argue it: the open blouse with a shading line between the breasts (both crops show it), and the fur running past the jaw down the throat (both crops show that too). The heart pendant came through on one seed of six.

*Logged 14:01.*

---

## 129. Abby character study, round 4, seed 55 - the ears finally read as a terrier's

![Abby character study, round 4, seed 55 - the ears finally read as a terrier's](images/129-abby-character-study-round-4-seed-55-the-ears-finally-read-as-a-terrier-s.png)

**The ask.** Round 4 of perfecting Abby. The judges called the references the disease, not the seeds: DROP canon/vision/studies/abby.png and canon/characters/abby/kit/bust.png outright - both draw the silky long coat, the wide lynx-tufted ears and the open-blouse cleavage that all six round-3 renders copied, and round 3 was still sending bust.png as Picture 2, which is where the Bichon came from. Replace them with crops of the plates Rick actually accepted, sc04 (470,360,820,900) and sc12 (450,350,830,930). Keep Picture 1 and the eye chart. Then fix, as EDIT lines: the eye pasted from the chart with a paper-white crescent at BOTH corners; the long fur stopping dead at the jaw; the closed-lip smile with no gap; the gaze on Drew and never on the reader; the harsh short double coat at a third the length; the broad flat-topped chrysanthemum head and blunt rectangular bearded muzzle; the small close-set erect triangular ears; the furred neck carrying the collar; the heavy black studded collar with a heart pendant; the buttoned blouse over a modest chest; grey modelling under the jaw; the back bar in wood grain with legible bottles; a clean straight edge on all four sides. And Drew, in the same plate: downy dash-strokes instead of scales, one S-curve with a single reversal, a heavy-lidded human eye. Hold the recipe - cfg 1 is correct - and re-run the same six seeds for a clean A/B.

**The thinking.** THE REFERENCE SWAP IS THE ROUND. bust.png and studies/abby.png are gone; Picture 2 is now the two head-and-shoulders crops of Rick's accepted plates in one tile. ONE FORCED DEVIATION FROM THE ORDER: the judges asked for the sc12 crop as a PICTURE 4, but the qwen family's max_refs is 3 on the bridge (backend/providers/local_bridge.py) and it drops extras off the END of the list, so a Picture 4 would have been binned on the wire and the round would have LOOKED like it tested the sc12 head without ever sending it. Both ordered crops therefore travel side by side in ONE tile, at the judges' own boxes, and both are genuinely on the wire. SECOND DEVIATION: four legible bottle labels inside the sc04 crop (EAGLE EYE VODKA and its neighbours) were blanked to plain paper first - round 3 proved at seed 55 that lettering inside a reference is copied and then elaborated into pseudo-text at cfg 1, which check 17 calls fatal - and canon asks a study's labels to be blank panels anyway. A BUG WAS ALSO FIXED: --cast-count was parsed and written into the sidecar but never reached the prompt, so round 3 told the model 'EXACTLY 1 character' while its Picture 1 was a three-character plate and its own EDITS said to keep Drew and Barclay; the header now says 3. Seed 55 first, as in round 3.

**Settings.** local/qwen-image-edit-2511, 4:5 1344x1680, fast Lightning 8 steps, cfg 1, euler/simple, shift 3.0, 3 references (Picture 1 the approved blank plate canon/plates/trio.png cut x60..1140 y20..1370, unchanged from round 3; Picture 2 NEW - the sc04 and sc12 head-and-shoulders crops of the plates Rick accepted, laid side by side in one tile; Picture 3 the hand-drawn eye chart, kept), scripts/cast-study.py --cast-count 3 --keep-duo with nineteen numbered EDITS, seed 55

**Prompt.** [prompts/129-abby-character-study-round-4-seed-55-the-ears-finally-read-as-a-terrier-s.prompt.txt](prompts/129-abby-character-study-round-4-seed-55-the-ears-finally-read-as-a-terrier-s.prompt.txt)

**Verdict.** RUNNER-UP OF SIX. The cleanest EAR read of the round and the thing the reference swap was for: two small hard-edged triangles carried erect and close-set on top of a broad flat skull, with the topknot between them, where round 3 gave wide-set tufted lapdog ears. The eye is second-best of the round - 24.6% below L60 with white measured at 253 and 253 against a paper of 254, so there is exact paper-white at both sides of the iris - and both pupils sit LEFT, onto Drew, so the founder's oldest complaint about her is finally answered on this seed. Nothing is lettered but the mirrored window; slate blank, every bottle label a blank panel. Marked below seed 44 on two counts: the back bar falls back into coarse dark hatch instead of the wood grain the EDIT asks for, and the bottom-left border breaks into spiked hatch fringe where the EDIT asks for a clean cut. The shared faults of the round are all here too - the lip parts on a gap, the coat runs past the jaw onto the throat, the blouse gapes.

*Logged 14:01.*

## 131. Abby character study, round 4, seed 7 - the hardest coat and a fourth ear

![Abby character study, round 4, seed 7 - the hardest coat and a fourth ear](images/131-abby-character-study-round-4-seed-7-the-hardest-coat-and-a-fourth-ear.png)

**The ask.** Round 4 of perfecting Abby. The judges called the references the disease, not the seeds: DROP canon/vision/studies/abby.png and canon/characters/abby/kit/bust.png outright - both draw the silky long coat, the wide lynx-tufted ears and the open-blouse cleavage that all six round-3 renders copied, and round 3 was still sending bust.png as Picture 2, which is where the Bichon came from. Replace them with crops of the plates Rick actually accepted, sc04 (470,360,820,900) and sc12 (450,350,830,930). Keep Picture 1 and the eye chart. Then fix, as EDIT lines: the eye pasted from the chart with a paper-white crescent at BOTH corners; the long fur stopping dead at the jaw; the closed-lip smile with no gap; the gaze on Drew and never on the reader; the harsh short double coat at a third the length; the broad flat-topped chrysanthemum head and blunt rectangular bearded muzzle; the small close-set erect triangular ears; the furred neck carrying the collar; the heavy black studded collar with a heart pendant; the buttoned blouse over a modest chest; grey modelling under the jaw; the back bar in wood grain with legible bottles; a clean straight edge on all four sides. And Drew, in the same plate: downy dash-strokes instead of scales, one S-curve with a single reversal, a heavy-lidded human eye. Hold the recipe - cfg 1 is correct - and re-run the same six seeds for a clean A/B.

**The thinking.** THE REFERENCE SWAP IS THE ROUND. bust.png and studies/abby.png are gone; Picture 2 is now the two head-and-shoulders crops of Rick's accepted plates in one tile. ONE FORCED DEVIATION FROM THE ORDER: the judges asked for the sc12 crop as a PICTURE 4, but the qwen family's max_refs is 3 on the bridge (backend/providers/local_bridge.py) and it drops extras off the END of the list, so a Picture 4 would have been binned on the wire and the round would have LOOKED like it tested the sc12 head without ever sending it. Both ordered crops therefore travel side by side in ONE tile, at the judges' own boxes, and both are genuinely on the wire. SECOND DEVIATION: four legible bottle labels inside the sc04 crop (EAGLE EYE VODKA and its neighbours) were blanked to plain paper first - round 3 proved at seed 55 that lettering inside a reference is copied and then elaborated into pseudo-text at cfg 1, which check 17 calls fatal - and canon asks a study's labels to be blank panels anyway. A BUG WAS ALSO FIXED: --cast-count was parsed and written into the sidecar but never reached the prompt, so round 3 told the model 'EXACTLY 1 character' while its Picture 1 was a three-character plate and its own EDITS said to keep Drew and Barclay; the header now says 3.

**Settings.** local/qwen-image-edit-2511, 4:5 1344x1680, fast Lightning 8 steps, cfg 1, euler/simple, shift 3.0, 3 references (Picture 1 the approved blank plate canon/plates/trio.png cut x60..1140 y20..1370, unchanged from round 3; Picture 2 NEW - the sc04 and sc12 head-and-shoulders crops of the plates Rick accepted, laid side by side in one tile; Picture 3 the hand-drawn eye chart, kept), scripts/cast-study.py --cast-count 3 --keep-duo with nineteen numbered EDITS, seed 7

**Prompt.** [prompts/131-abby-character-study-round-4-seed-7-the-hardest-coat-and-a-fourth-ear.prompt.txt](prompts/131-abby-character-study-round-4-seed-7-the-hardest-coat-and-a-fourth-ear.prompt.txt)

**Verdict.** FOURTH. It draws the SHORTEST, HARDEST coat of the six and is the closest any render came to the harsh double coat the EDIT asks for - short straight strokes with tonal breaks, rather than the long silky sweep the other seeds kept. The eye is clean at 22.6% dark with white at 231 and 226 on a paper of 241. Two faults hold it down. FOUR EAR FORMS: extra ear-shaped tufts of fur stand outside both real ears, so the head reads with four pricked shapes instead of two. And the lip line opens on a visible tooth right of centre, where the EDIT says the lips touch along their whole length.

*Logged 14:01.*

**Verdict.** BEST OF THE SIX, and the best Abby of the whole task so far. THE GAZE IS OFF THE LENS: both pupils sit left, onto Drew, both eyes are on the page and the far eye is about four fifths the near one's width, so the head turn reads without losing an eye. THE BREED IS RIGHT: broad flat-topped skull wider than it is tall, two small close-set erect triangular ears and nothing extra, a short blunt muzzle with its beard fringe, a heavy black studded collar sitting up on the neck. THE ROOM IS RIGHT: back bar in readable wood grain with legible bottle silhouettes and clear glass, the counter crossing her at the waist, both hands working the glass and the towel, Drew and Barclay seated on the near side, the slate blank, every label a blank panel with no letterform anywhere. FOUR FAULTS REMAIN, and they are shared by every seed, so they are language and canon faults rather than seed faults: (1) the lip line still opens a dark gap; (2) the long coat still runs past the jaw down the throat and into the blouse; (3) the blouse still gapes with a shading line between the breasts; (4) the pendant is an oval, not the heart. The bottom-left border also frays into hatch fringe, which only seed 41 avoided.

*Logged 14:01.*

Then fix, as EDIT lines: the eye pasted from the chart with a paper-white crescent at BOTH corners; the long fur stopping dead at the jaw; the closed-lip smile with no gap; the gaze on Drew and never on the reader; the harsh short double coat at a third the length; the broad flat-topped chrysanthemum head and blunt rectangular bearded muzzle; the small close-set erect triangular ears; the furred neck carrying the collar; the heavy black studded collar with a heart pendant; the buttoned blouse over a modest chest; grey modelling under the jaw; the back bar in wood grain with legible bottles; a clean straight edge on all four sides. And Drew, in the same plate: downy dash-strokes instead of scales, one S-curve with a single reversal, a heavy-lidded human eye. Hold the recipe - cfg 1 is correct - and re-run the same six seeds for a clean A/B.

**The thinking.** THE REFERENCE SWAP IS THE ROUND. bust.png and studies/abby.png are gone; Picture 2 is now the two head-and-shoulders crops of Rick's accepted plates in one tile. ONE FORCED DEVIATION FROM THE ORDER: the judges asked for the sc12 crop as a PICTURE 4, but the qwen family's max_refs is 3 on the bridge (backend/providers/local_bridge.py) and it drops extras off the END of the list, so a Picture 4 would have been binned on the wire and the round would have LOOKED like it tested the sc12 head without ever sending it. Both ordered crops therefore travel side by side in ONE tile, at the judges' own boxes, and both are genuinely on the wire. SECOND DEVIATION: four legible bottle labels inside the sc04 crop (EAGLE EYE VODKA and its neighbours) were blanked to plain paper first - round 3 proved at seed 55 that lettering inside a reference is copied and then elaborated into pseudo-text at cfg 1, which check 17 calls fatal - and canon asks a study's labels to be blank panels anyway. A BUG WAS ALSO FIXED: --cast-count was parsed and written into the sidecar but never reached the prompt, so round 3 told the model 'EXACTLY 1 character' while its Picture 1 was a three-character plate and its own EDITS said to keep Drew and Barclay; the header now says 3. The sheet puts the six renders beside the two kit tiles this round dropped and the three references that were actually sent, so the swap can be judged by eye in one look: the old tiles draw a silky long-coated lapdog with wide tufted ears, the accepted plates draw a hard-coated terrier with small ears set close on top, and the renders now follow the second. Every panel carries its measured near-eye numbers from scripts/measure-abby-eye.py, so the eye is judged by number as the judges asked and not by impression.

**Settings.** PIL, 1702x2372 greyscale; scripts/build-abby-round4-sheet.py

**Prompt.** [prompts/135-abby-round-4-the-contact-sheet-six-seeds-against-the-tiles-that-were-dropped.prompt.txt](prompts/135-abby-round-4-the-contact-sheet-six-seeds-against-the-tiles-that-were-dropped.prompt.txt)

**Verdict.** THE ROUND IN ONE PICTURE. WON: the breed. The Bichon is gone 6 of 6 - flat-topped skull, small close-set triangular ears, blunt bearded muzzle in every render, against 0 of 6 in round 3. WON: the eye, by number - the near eye measures 22.0-26.8% below L60 across the five good seeds, and seed 44 puts exact paper white (254/254 on a paper of 254) at both sides of the iris. WON: the gaze, in 4 of 6 - the pupils sit left onto Drew where all six of round 3 stared at the reader. WON: the back bar, now wood grain with legible bottles in 4 of 6. LOST: the jawline, 6 of 6 - the coat still crosses onto the throat. LOST: the closed lip, 6 of 6. LOST: the modest blouse, 6 of 6. LOST: all three of Drew's fixes, 6 of 6 - his neck is still drawn in overlapping scales, still one unbroken C-hook, and only his EYE improved, which is the one Drew fix that landed. The reason is visible in the prompt: the auto-appended tail EDIT tells the model that everything other than the studied character stays exactly as Picture 1, and Picture 1 draws the scaled neck. Drew needs his own pass, or a corrected Picture 1. And three of the four remaining Abby faults are canon's own fence wording ('open two buttons over a smooth sleek throat', 'a teardrop gem pendant', 'a round soft fluffy westie head') being read AFTER the EDITS that contradict it - the fence, not the prompt, is now the thing fighting the judges.

*Logged 14:01.*

## 136. DREW, perfected as a standalone study with the house recipe: a flamingo - round 4, seed 62, the best of 24 judged renders

![DREW, perfected as a standalone study with the house recipe: a flamingo - round 4, seed 62, the best of 24 judged renders](images/136-drew-perfected-as-a-standalone-study-with-the-house-recipe-a-flamingo-round-4-seed-62-the-best-of-24-judged-re.png)

**The ask.** its a flamingo you did a terrible job you must perfect each character

**The thinking.** The swans came from rendering the cast with the room's model from crude block-ins. The plates Rick approved were drawn by the house edit model with each character's official portrait as the picture to copy identically, through the studio's own prompt builder - so that recipe was rebuilt as scripts/cast-study.py and Drew was rendered as a standalone study, six seeds a round, judged every round by a checklist judge and an adversarial eye against the bible and the set Rick accepted. By round four he is a flamingo: the S-neck with one reversal, the heavy bill bending down to its black outer third, the heavy-lidded human eye, plumage in fine strokes, the knit vest with ribbed armholes, collar and bow tie, the martini. The judges still want the bill's highlight ribbon, a sharper flamingo bend two-thirds along, one contour arc above the eye and a touch more white in the eye's corners.

**Settings.** local/qwen-image-edit-2511, 1344x1680 (4:5), fast 8-step cfg 1; Picture 1 = Drew's crop of canon/plates/duo.png, Picture 2 = canon/vision/studies/drew.png; LOCAL fence + numbered EDITS

**Verdict.** shown to the founder; judges: close, four fixes left

*Logged 14:08.*
